| `20260815.223938` | [`20260815-223938_crypto-vault-seal-password-box.bron`](20260815/20260815-223938_crypto-vault-seal-password-box.bron) | **Season G twenty-first rung — the vault seal, a password-sealed box (the third composition)** — the next Lindy-first crux after Argon2: `crypto/vault_seal.rye` composes the already-GREEN `crypto/argon2.rye` (Argon2id) and `crypto/xchacha20.rye` (XChaCha20-Poly1305) into a box that locks a blob behind a **remembered password**, so the key that opens it need never live on the device — authoring **no new cryptography**. Where the signed carry answers *who made this record* and the sealed session answers *only you can read this* to a public key, the vault seal answers *only your password can open this* — the exact **Vault** sealed-storage shape both primitives were built toward. The caller supplies a fresh random salt and a fresh random 24-byte nonce per seal (the library invents no entropy); Argon2id stretches the password against the salt at the caller's chosen cost into a 32-byte key, and XChaCha20-Poly1305 seals under it with the whole public header (magic, cost, salt, nonce) bound as **authenticated data**, so a tampered salt or cost cannot trick open into a silent mis-derive. Frame: magic `GVS1` ‖ mode ‖ t ‖ m ‖ p ‖ salt-len ‖ salt ‖ nonce ‖ cipher-len(u64 BE) ‖ ciphertext ‖ Poly1305 tag. **GREEN on the first metal pass**: round-trip across seven plaintext lengths (empty through 64 KiB), the ciphertext provably not the plaintext, byte-identical determinism, the fresh-salt property (a different salt yields a different frame that still opens under the same password), and **seven refusals** — wrong password, tampered ciphertext/tag/salt/cost all `OpenFailed` with no plaintext, plus bad magic, a bad mode byte, two truncations, an undersized buffer, and a too-short and too-long salt. The derived key is wiped from the stack after use as best-effort hygiene, not a timing claim. Registered last in `crypto_suite_witness.rish` — **whole-suite witness GREEN across all twenty-one files** (eighteen primitives + three compositions); `tame_style_check` bans clean, the new file in no ratchet list; `crypto/README.md` carries the new row. Non-gated — TEST passwords, no real secret, no network, no funds, no device; constant-time timing-safety stays the Season's named **horizon** and sealing a real secret behind a real password stays the **custody gate**. |

| `20260815.214352` | [`20260815-214352_season-g-xchacha20-extended-nonce-aead.bron`](20260815/20260815-214352_season-g-xchacha20-extended-nonce-aead.bron) | **Season G — HChaCha20 and the XChaCha20-Poly1305 extended-nonce AEAD, parity-GREEN** — the next Lindy-first crux on the crypto thread: the seventeenth file, `crypto/xchacha20.rye`, widening ChaCha20-Poly1305's nonce to 24 bytes so a keeper can draw a nonce at **random** and stay safe (the 12-byte IETF nonce is too small for that) — what Vault sealed storage and a long-lived Comlink session want, and the construction **Monocypher's own crypto_aead_lock** uses. HChaCha20 is ChaCha20's permutation stopped one step early — same state fill, same twenty rounds, **no feed-forward**, serializing words 0..3 and 12..15 into a 256-bit subkey; XChaCha20-Poly1305 derives subkey = HChaCha20(key, nonce[0..16]) and subnonce = 0x00000000 ‖ nonce[16..24], then runs our own GREEN AEAD. Stands entirely on proven stones — one small new core plus the composed AEAD, **no new field math, no new authenticator**. Parity holds against **three** references: the draft §2.2.1 HChaCha20 subkey KAT, a **published external** Zig std.crypto XChaCha20-Poly1305 AEAD known-answer, and byte-for-byte parity vs Zig across sizes and aad lengths; plus nonce-separation, four tamper refusals, and bounds refusals. Registered in the suite (after aead) — **whole-suite witness GREEN across all seventeen files**, each rebuilt fresh; `tame_style_check` bans clean. A first-run RED (a mis-transcribed subkey literal, not a code bug) was caught by running Zig's own hchacha standalone before assuming fault, then anchored on an external published value. Non-gated — TEST vectors only, no real identity key, no network, no funds, no device; signing or agreeing with the maintainer's own key stays the **custody gate**. |

| `20260815.205254` | [`20260815-205254_season-g-sealed-session.bron`](20260815/20260815-205254_season-g-sealed-session.bron) | **Season G sixteenth rung — the sealed session, the SECOND composition over the GREEN primitives (Comlink confidentiality)** — where the signed carry answers "who made this record" in the clear, this answers "only you can read this." `crypto/sealed_session.rye` composes **X25519** (key agreement), **BLAKE2b-512** (the KDF), and **ChaCha20-Poly1305** (authenticated encryption) into a sealed session carry that sends one keeper a **confidential** message over an open channel, authoring **no new cryptography**. The standard seal-to-a-public-key box: for each message the sender takes a **fresh ephemeral X25519 keypair** (the caller supplies fresh randomness), agrees a one-message shared secret with the recipient's public key, and derives **both** a 32-byte session key **and** a 12-byte nonce from one BLAKE2b-512 over `(shared ‖ ephemeral-pub ‖ recipient-pub)` — so the AEAD nonce is **never reused** under a key, and the recipient's own key is bound as **authenticated data** so a frame sealed to one keeper cannot be replayed to another. A frame is `magic GSS1 ‖ ephemeral pubkey ‖ cipher-len (u64 BE) ‖ ciphertext ‖ Poly1305 tag`; `open()` rederives the secret from the recipient's private key and returns plaintext **only when the tag verifies**. **GREEN on the first metal pass**: round-trip across **seven** plaintext lengths (empty through 64 KiB), the ciphertext provably **not** the plaintext, the **sealed-box freshness** property (two ephemerals yield different frames that both open to the same message), byte-identical determinism given a fixed ephemeral, and **six refusals** — a wrong recipient, a tampered ciphertext, a tampered tag, and a tampered ephemeral key all fail **OpenFailed** revealing no plaintext, plus bad magic and two truncations and an undersized buffer. `tame_style` bans clean, the new file in none of the ratchet lists. Purely local over **TEST keypairs** — no real identity key, no network, no funds, no real device; the ephemeral private must be fresh randomness in real use, constant-time timing-safety stays the named **horizon**, and agreeing a session with the maintainer's own identity key stays the **custody gate**. Comlink's confidential session now stands as a library on primitives already parity-GREEN. |
| `20260815.204736` | [`20260815-204736_season-g-signed-carry.bron`](20260815/20260815-204736_season-g-signed-carry.bron) | **Season G fifteenth rung — the signed carry, the FIRST composition over the GREEN Monocypher-parity primitives** — the fourteen rungs before this authored the primitives; this one composes two of them into the shape every module that records provenance reaches for. `crypto/signed_carry.rye` builds a self-describing, content-addressed, Ed25519-signed carry frame purely from **BLAKE2b-512** (the content address) and **Ed25519 sign/verify** (the seal), authoring **no new cryptography**. | `20260815.184832` | [`20260815-184832_sha3-preference-post-quantum-kumara.bron`](20260815/20260815-184832_sha3-preference-post-quantum-kumara.bron) | **SHA-3 preferred, seated; post-quantum Kumara proposed; one quantum premise corrected** — Keaton asked to prioritize SHA-3 over SHA-2 and, if Ed25519 rests on SHA-2, to choose a quantum-resistant SHA3-512 curve for Kumara. **Seated:** prefer SHA-3/SHAKE for new designs (sponge · no length-extension · distinct lineage) — with the honesty guardrail that **SHA-2 is not broken** (SHA-512 has no practical collision/preimage attack; the breaks were SHA-1/MD5), so SHA-3 is a stronger-design preference, never a false vulnerability claim; SHA-512 stays for Monocypher/Ed25519 parity. **Corrected, honestly:** no elliptic curve is quantum-resistant — Shor breaks the curve regardless of hash — and 512-bit hashes are already quantum-safe (Grover only halves → ~256-bit); the quantum threat is the *curve*, not the *hash*. | `20260815.014021` | [`20260815-014021_ales115-lotus-replace.bron`](20260815/20260815-014021_ales115-lotus-replace.bron) | **ALES115 — Lotus's replace: remove `count` samples and insert `src` in their place IN PLACE, the general span-edit that ties cut, splice, and paste_over into one law with three faces** — `replace(clip, at, count, src)` is exactly ALES2's `cut(at, count)` then `splice(at, src)` composed over its own public API, inventing no third primitive. | `20260815.001849` | [`20260815-001849_ales103-lotus-peak-normalize.bron`](20260815/20260815-001849_ales103-lotus-peak-normalize.bron) | **ALES103 — Lotus's peak normalize: measure the loudest sample through ALES13's meter, then scale the whole clip through ALES2's gain so it lands exactly on a chosen target — the Normalize button every editor ships** — the silence family (ALES96–102) answered *where is the sound?*; this rung answers what a keeper asks the instant the sound is found: *how loud is it, and can every take be the same loudness?* A take recorded quiet and a take recorded hot both leave the same peak after normalize, so a keeper mixes without fighting the input level, and it is the first Lotus rung to **fuse the reader (ALES13's meter) and the writer (ALES2's gain)** into one tool. `normalize_peak(clip, target)` — `target` a magnitude in `[1, sample_max]` — measures the clip's peak through ALES13's `measure`; a silent clip (peak 0) is left **exactly as it is** (no peak to lift, the guard that also keeps the division from ever dividing by zero); otherwise it scales the whole clip `[0, clip.len)` by `target/peak` through ALES2's saturating `gain`, amplifying a quiet clip and attenuating a hot one with the **same** fraction. The crux is **four laws**: the **scale law** — normalize is nothing but `measure` then `gain(clip, 0, len, target, peak)`, inventing no new arithmetic (a peak of 8000 to 16000 a clean ×2, a peak of 30000 to 15000 a clean ×0.5), so it can never drift; the **peak law** — after normalize the clip's peak magnitude equals `target` **exactly** for any non-silent clip at any target (the peak sample maps to exactly ±target, every quieter sample strictly inside it), proven including the i16 minimum at the meter's domain ceiling; the **silence law** — an all-zero clip and an empty clip are unchanged whatever the target; the **idempotence law** — normalize twice to one target equals once, the peak already on the target so the second gain is unity. Because the peak lands exactly on `target ≤ sample_max`, no sample ever actually saturates. A zero target and a target past `sample_max` each refuse `BadTarget`, the clip untouched. Its error union is the meter's `MeterError` and the editor's `EditError` (both honestly unreachable here — a bounded Clip, a measured non-zero denominator, a whole-clip span) reused by name plus `BadTarget`. Four laws proven (peak-law grid 25); witness `tools/al/ales_normalize_witness.rish` GREEN, TAME clean. Purely local — it changes sample **values** only through `gain`, fabricating none and never a length; a plain sample magnitude, **not** a decibel or a loudness model (peak normalization, the plainest reading); no real sample rate, no network, no keys, no funds. Design read `active-designing/date/20260815/20260815-001849_fill-ales103-lotus-peak-normalize.md`; front door `lotus/README.md` synced. |
| `20260815.001200` | [`20260815-001200_ales102-lotus-smoothed-silence-trim.bron`](20260815/20260815-001200_ales102-lotus-smoothed-silence-trim.bron) | **ALES102 — Lotus's smoothed silence trim: smooth the runs through ALES101 before stripping the silence, so the strip declicks the pause and keeps the gap — where ALES101's hysteresis pays off** — ALES96 strips every `.silent` run from the **raw** segmentation, and the raw segmentation flickers: a single loud tick buried in a pause reads `.voiced`, so ALES96 **keeps the click**; a single quiet frame between two words reads `.silent`, so ALES96 **cuts the gap** and the words run together. Run ALES101 *before* the strip and both vanish — the tick, shorter than `min_run`, is relabeled to the silence around it and stripped; the short gap, shorter than `min_run`, is relabeled to the speech around it and **kept**. One knob turns a literal silence remover into a speech-aware one: remove the dead air, spare the breath, drop the click. `smooth_trim(clip, frame_len, t_low, t_high, silence_floor, voice_split, min_run)` is the ALES96 shape with one stage inserted — segment `[0, clip.len)` through ALES95, **smooth through ALES101**, then cut the smoothed `.silent` runs back to front (ALES101's coverage law guaranteeing every smoothed silent run is a valid original span). The crux is **four laws**: the **min-zero law** — `smooth_trim(…, 0)` equals ALES96's `trim_silence` byte for byte (identity smoothing, a strict generalization); the **concat law** — the result equals the hand-built concatenation of the non-silent *smoothed* runs, so it can never drift; the **declick law** — a short `.voiced` tick buried in silence (silent 8 · voiced 4 · silent 8) is removed at `min_run 8`, strictly **shorter** than the raw trim, the click gone; the **keep-gap law** — a short `.silent` gap between two words (voiced 8 · silent 4 · voiced 8) is kept at `min_run 8`, strictly **longer** than the raw trim, the breath preserved. Honestly named: `smooth_trim` is **not monotone** in `min_run` — a larger `min_run` removes more clicks yet keeps more gaps, the declick and keep-gap laws its two sides. An all-silent clip empties, a no-silent clip is unchanged, and an empty clip, a ragged frame length, an inverted band, and an illegal floor each refuse by name (`BadFrame`, `BadFrame`, `BadThreshold`, `BadFloor`), the clip untouched. Its error union is ALES96's `TrimError` with ALES101's `SmoothError` reused by name. Four laws proven (min-zero grid 96, concat grid 8); witness `tools/al/ales_smooth_trim_witness.rish` GREEN, TAME clean, width-check clean. Purely local — it edits only by removing whole smoothed-silent frames through `cut`, fabricating no samples and reordering nothing; a causal hold, **not** an adaptive VAD; no real sample rate, no network, no keys, no funds. Design read `active-designing/date/20260815/20260815-001200_fill-ales102-lotus-smoothed-silence-trim.md`; front door `lotus/README.md` synced. |
| `20260815.000323` | [`20260815-000323_ales101-lotus-run-length-smoother.bron`](20260815/20260815-000323_ales101-lotus-run-length-smoother.bron) | **ALES101 — Lotus's run-length smoother: absorb every run shorter than a minimum into what surrounds it — the smoothing hysteresis ALES95's own scope note named as a later rung** — raw per-frame segmentation flickers: one loud tick inside a pause reads as a one-frame `.voiced` blip, one quiet frame inside speech as a spurious `.silent` gap. Feed that flicker to ALES96's stripper or ALES100's collapser and it splits a pause in two, or spares a click as if it were a word. Every VAD, gate, and auto-splitter fixes it the same way: a region must last a minimum duration to count, anything shorter absorbed into what surrounds it. `smooth_runs(in, out, min_run)` is a **read-only transform over the labels** — a rewrite of one `segment.Segments` into a smoothed one, **not one PCM sample touched**, keeping the family a tower of readers. The rule: seed the output with the first run, then for each later run extend the open run (keeping its verdict) when the run is shorter than `min_run` **or** shares the open run's verdict, else push it — a short excursion is relabeled to the surrounding sound and same-verdict neighbours re-coalesce in the same pass. The crux is **four laws checked as data**: **coverage** — the smoothed runs tile the same span exactly (same first start, same total length); **coalesced** — no two adjacent output runs share a verdict; the **hold** — every run past the first is at least `min_run`, the opener the honest exception a causal left-to-right hold cannot fix; **idempotence + monotonicity** — a second pass at the same `min_run` is a fixed point, a larger `min_run` yields at most as many runs. The identity falls out: `min_run = 0` (and any `min_run` below the shortest run) leaves a coalesced input unchanged. A spurious short blip is absorbed and its neighbours re-coalesce (silent 100 · voiced 2 · silent 100 → one silent 202); a leading short run honestly survives; a `min_run` past the whole span coalesces to one; an empty input stays empty and a single run is unchanged. Its only error `SmoothError.SegmentsFull` is kept named for the honest path though it cannot fire (`out.len ≤ in.len ≤ max_segments`), the same honesty ALES95 keeps. Four laws proven (crux grid 27, nine `min_run` values); witness `tools/al/ales_smooth_runs_witness.rish` GREEN, TAME clean, width-check clean. Purely local, read-only over the labels — a causal left-to-right hold, **not** the symmetric smoothing or adaptive floors a production VAD would add; **not** a transcript or a diarization; no real sample rate, no network, no keys, no funds. Design read `active-designing/date/20260815/20260815-000323_fill-ales101-lotus-run-length-smoother.md`; front door `lotus/README.md` synced. |
